You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **papers**:\n    27→  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n    28→  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n    29→  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n    30→- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    31→- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n    32→- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n    33→- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n    34→\n    35→## Decoder Task\n    36→\n    37→Temporally align based on stimulus onset\n    38→\n    39→### Decoder Inputs:\n    40→- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n    41→- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n    42→\n    43→### Decoder Outputs\n    44→- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n    45→- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n    46→- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    47→- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    48→\n    49→## Target Data Format\n    50→\n    51→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    52→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    53→\n    54→```python\n    55→data = {\n    56→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    57→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    58→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    59→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    60→            ...\n    61→        ],\n    62→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    77→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    78→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    79→            ...\n    80→        ],\n    81→        ...\n    82→    ],\n    83→\n    84→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    85→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    86→\n    87→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    88→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    89→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    90→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    91→\n    92→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    93→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    94→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    95→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    96→        ...\n    97→    ],\n    98→\n    99→    'metadata': {\n   100→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   101→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   102→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   103→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   104→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   105→        ...\n   106→    }\n   107→}\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→### Key Format Requirements\n   111→\n   112→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   113→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   114→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   115→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   116→\n   117→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   118→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   119→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   120→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   121→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   122→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   123→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   124→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   125→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   126→\n   127→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   128→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   129→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   130→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   131→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   132→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   135→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   136→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   137→\n   138→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   139→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   140→\n   141→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   142→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   143→\n   144→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   146→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   147→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   148→\n   149→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   150→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   151→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   152→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   153→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   154→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   155→\n   156→## Python environment\n   157→\n   158→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   159→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   160→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   161→\n   162→## Decoder Reference\n   163→\n   164→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   165→- Optional arguments:\n   166→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   167→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   168→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   169→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   170→\n   171→### Purpose\n   172→\n   173→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   174→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   175→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   176→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   177→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   178→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   179→\n   180→### Consistency requirements\n   181→\n   182→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   183→- Loading of data\n   184→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   185→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   186→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   187→\n   188→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   189→\n   190→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   191→\n   192→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   193→\n   194→Carefully verify your work after every step. (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   319→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   320→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   321→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   322→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   323→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   324→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   325→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   326→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   327→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   328→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   329→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   330→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   331→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   332→\n   333→**Done when**:\n   334→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   335→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   336→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   337→\n   338→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   339→\n   340→---\n   341→\n   342→### Step 6: Script Development\n   343→\n   344→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   345→\n   346→**Actions**:\n   347→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   348→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   349→- Include options:\n   350→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   351→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   352→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   353→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   354→- Write efficient code:\n   355→  - Vectorize loops\n   356→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   357→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   358→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   359→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   360→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   361→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   362→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   363→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   364→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   365→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   366→\n   367→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   368→\n   369→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   370→\n   371→---\n   372→\n   373→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   374→\n   375→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   376→\n   377→**Actions**:\n   378→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   379→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   380→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   381→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   382→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   383→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   384→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   385→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   386→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   387→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   388→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   389→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   390→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   391→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   392→\n   393→**Done when**: \n   394→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   395→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   396→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   397→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   398→\n   399→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   400→\n   401→---\n   402→\n   403→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   404→\n   405→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   406→\n   407→**Actions**:\n   408→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   409→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   410→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   411→\n   412→**Investigate any issues**:\n   413→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   414→\n   415→**Done when**: \n   416→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   417→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   418→- Accuracies are above chance\n   419→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   420→\n   421→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   422→\n   423→---\n   424→\n   425→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   426→\n   427→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   428→\n   429→**Actions**:\n   430→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   431→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   432→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   433→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   434→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   435→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   436→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   437→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   438→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   439→\n   440→**Done when**:\n   441→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   442→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   443→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   444→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   445→\n   446→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   447→\n   448→---\n   449→\n   450→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   451→\n   452→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   453→\n   454→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   455→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   456→\n   457→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   458→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   459→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   460→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   461→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   462→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   463→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   464→\n   465→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   466→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   467→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   468→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   469→- Fix any mismatches. \n   470→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   471→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   472→\n   473→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   474→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n   475→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   476→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   477→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   478→\n   479→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   480→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   481→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   482→\n   483→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   484→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   485→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   486→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   487→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   488→\n   489→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   490→\n   491→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   492→\n   493→---\n   494→\n   495→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   496→\n   497→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   498→\n   499→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   500→\n   501→**Actions**:\n   502→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   503→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   504→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   505→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n   506→\n   507→**Done when**: \n   508→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   509→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   510→\n   511→---\n   512→\n   513→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   514→\n   515→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   516→\n   517→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   518→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   519→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   520→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   521→\n   522→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n   523→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n   524→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers's accuracy for each variable\n   525→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   526→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   527→\n   528→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   529→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   530→\n   531→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   532→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   533→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   534→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   535→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   536→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n   537→\n   538→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   539→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   540→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   541→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   542→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   543→\n   544→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   545→\n   546→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   547→\n   548→---\n   549→\n   550→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   551→\n   552→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   553→\n   554→**Actions**:\n   555→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   556→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   557→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   558→  - Validation results and tables\n   559→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   560→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   561→  - Dataset description\n   562→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   563→  - Output format specification\n   564→  - Key statistics\n   565→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   566→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   567→\n   568→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   569→\n   570→---\n   571→\n   572→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   573→\n   574→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   826→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   827→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   828→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   829→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   830→\n   831→### Computational Efficiency\n   832→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   833→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   834→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   835→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   836→\n   837→## Success Criteria\n   838→\n   839→A successful conversion should:\n   840→1. Match the target data structure exactly\n   841→2. Preserve all relevant information from the source\n   842→3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n   843→4. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **papers**:\n  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. Optimized implementation.\"\"\"\n   228→    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n   229→    binned = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n   230→\n   231→    # Pre-sort spikes are already time-sorted after merge\n   232→    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n   233→        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n   234→        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n   235→\n   236→        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n   237→            continue\n   238→\n   239→        # Butterworth low-pass filter for velocity\n   269→    sos = signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_freq / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\n   270→    vel = np.insert(np.diff(signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos_interp)), 0, 0) * fs\n   271→\n   272→    return t, np.abs(vel).astype(np.float32)  # wheel speed = |velocity|\n   273→\n   274→\n   275→def load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left'):\n   276→    \"\"\"Load whisker motion energy from camera data.\"\"\"\n   277→    if side == 'left':\n   278→        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n   279→        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')\n   280→    else:\n   281→        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n   282→        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n   283→\n   284→    if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n   285→        return None, None\n   286→\n   287→    me = np.load(me_file).flatten()\n   288→    times = np.load(times_file).flatten()\n   289→\n   290→    # Find behavior data in this interval\n   309→        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_start, side='right')\n   310→        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n   311→\n   312→        t_sel = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   313→        v_sel = beh_values[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   314→\n   315→        if len(v_sel) == 0:\n   316→            continue\n   317→\n   318→        # Check data availability (matching reference code)\n   319→        if np.abs(t_start - t_sel[0]) > binsize:\n   320→            continue\n   321→        if np.abs(t_end - t_sel[-1]) > binsize:\n   322→            continue\n   323→\n   324→        # Interpolate to uniform bins (matching reference code)\n   325→        x_interp = np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n   326→        try:\n   327→            y_interp = interp1d(t_sel, v_sel, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n   328→            result[trial_idx] = y_interp\n   329→            good_mask[trial_idx] = True\n   330→        except Exception:\n   331→            continue\n   332→\n   333→    return result, good_mask\n   334→\n   335→\n   336→def discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=N_DISCRETE_BINS):\n   337→    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins equal-frequency bins using quantiles.\n   338→    Computed across all valid (non-NaN) values.\"\"\"\n   339→    valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n   340→    if len(valid) == 0:\n   341→        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=int), np.array([])\n   342→\n   343→    # Compute quantile boundaries\n   344→    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n   345→    boundaries = np.percentile(valid, quantiles)\n   346→\n   347→    # Digitize - np.digitize returns values 0..n_bins, clip to 0..n_bins-1\n   348→    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries[1:-1])  # 0 to n_bins-1\n   349→    result = np.clip(result, 0, n_bins - 1)\n   350→\n   351→    return result.astype(int), boundaries\n   352→\n   353→\n   354→def compute_trial_in_block(prob_left):\n   355→    \"\"\"Compute trial number within each block.\n   356→    Block boundaries are where probabilityLeft changes.\"\"\"\n   357→    trial_in_block = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n   358→    count = 1\n   359→    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n   360→        if i > 0 and prob_left[i] != prob_left[i-1]:\n   361→            count = 1\n   362→        trial_in_block[i] = count\n   363→        count += 1\n   364→    return trial_in_block\n   365→\n   366→\n   367→def process_session(session_info, br, show_processing=False):\n   368→    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\"\"\"\n   369→    eid = session_info['eid']\n   370→    subject = session_info['subject']\n   371→    lab = session_info['lab']\n   372→    date = session_info['date']\n   373→\n   374→    t0 = time.time()\n   375→\n   376→    # Find session path\n   377→    alf_path = find_session_path(lab, subject, date)\n   378→    if alf_path is None:\n   379→        print(f\"  Session {eid} ({subject}/{date}): path not found, skipping\")\n   380→        return None\n   381→\n   382→    # Load trials\n   383→    try:\n   384→        trials = load_trials(alf_path)\n   385→    except Exception as e:\n   386→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading trials: {e}\")\n   387→        return None\n   388→\n   389→    # Create trial mask\n   390→    mask = create_trial_mask(trials)\n   391→\n   392→    # Find probes\n   393→    probe_dirs = sorted([d.name for d in alf_path.iterdir() if d.is_dir() and d.name.startswith('probe')])\n   394→    if not probe_dirs:\n   395→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: no probes found, skipping\")\n   396→        return None\n   397→\n   398→    # Load and merge spike sorting from all probes\n   399→    spikes_list = []\n   400→    clusters_list = []\n   401→    for probe_name in probe_dirs:\n   402→        try:\n   403→            spk, clu = load_spike_sorting(alf_path, probe_name)\n   404→            spikes_list.append(spk)\n   405→            clusters_list.append(clu)\n   406→        except Exception as e:\n   407→            print(f\"  Session {eid}, {probe_name}: error loading spikes: {e}\")\n   408→            continue\n   409→\n   410→    if not spikes_list:\n   411→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: no valid probes, skipping\")\n   412→        return None\n   413→\n   414→    spikes, clusters = merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)\n   415→    n_clusters = len(clusters['channels'])\n   416→\n   417→    # Get brain regions\n   418→    cluster_regions = get_brain_regions(clusters, br)\n   419→\n   420→    # Apply trial mask\n   421→    valid_trials = trials[mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n   422→\n   423→    if len(valid_trials) < 2:\n   424→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: fewer than 2 valid trials, skipping\")\n   425→        return None\n   426→\n   427→    # Compute intervals aligned to stimOn_times\n   428→    align_times = valid_trials[ALIGN_TIME].values\n   429→    interval_starts = align_times + TIME_WINDOW[0]\n   430→    interval_ends = align_times + TIME_WINDOW[1]\n   431→\n   432→    # Bin spikes\n   433→    binned_spikes = bin_spikes_vectorized(\n   434→        spikes['times'], spikes['clusters'], n_clusters,\n   435→        interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n   436→    )\n   437→    # binned_spikes shape: (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n   438→\n   439→    # Load wheel data\n   440→    wheel_speed_binned = None\n   441→    try:\n   442→        wheel_pos_raw = np.load(find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_wheel.position.npy')).flatten()\n   443→        wheel_ts_raw = np.load(find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy')).flatten()\n   444→        wheel_times, wheel_speed = interpolate_wheel(wheel_ts_raw, wheel_pos_raw)\n   445→        wheel_speed_binned, wheel_good = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n   446→            wheel_times, wheel_speed, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n   447→        )\n   448→    except Exception as e:\n   449→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading wheel: {e}\")\n   450→\n   451→    # Load whisker motion energy\n   452→    me_binned = None\n   453→    try:\n   454→        me_times, me_values = load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left')\n   455→        if me_times is None:\n   456→            me_times, me_values = load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='right')\n   457→        if me_times is not None:\n   458→            me_binned, me_good = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n   459→                me_times, me_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n   460→            )\n   461→    except Exception as e:\n   462→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading motion energy: {e}\")\n   463→\n   464→    # Build combined mask (trials where all data is available)\n   465→    combined_mask = np.ones(len(valid_trials), dtype=bool)\n   466→    if wheel_speed_binned is not None:\n   467→        combined_mask &= ~np.any(np.isnan(wheel_speed_binned), axis=1)\n   468→    else:\n   469→        # If no wheel data, mark all as bad\n   470→        combined_mask[:] = False\n   471→\n   472→    if me_binned is not None:\n   473→        combined_mask &= ~np.any(np.isnan(me_binned), axis=1)\n   474→    else:\n   475→        combined_mask[:] = False\n   476→\n   477→    # Apply combined mask\n   478→    n_valid = combined_mask.sum()\n   479→    if n_valid < 2:\n   480→        print(f\"  Session {eid}: fewer than 2 trials with all data, skipping\")\n   481→        return None\n   482→\n   483→    final_spikes = binned_spikes[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n   484→    final_wheel = wheel_speed_binned[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n   485→    final_me = me_binned[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n   486→    final_trials = valid_trials[combined_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n   487→\n   488→    # Build neural data: list of (n_neurons, n_timepoints) per trial\n   489→    # Use uint8 to save memory (spike counts per 20ms bin are small integers, max ~12)\n   490→    neural_trials = []\n   491→    for t in range(len(final_spikes)):\n   492→        neural_trials.append(final_spikes[t].astype(np.uint8))  # (n_clusters, n_bins)\n   493→\n   494→    # Build inputs\n   495→    # Input 0: time since stimulus onset (continuous, time-varying)\n   496→    time_since_stim = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS).astype(np.float32)\n   497→\n   498→    # Input 1: trial number in block (continuous, per-trial)\n   499→    prob_left_all = trials['probabilityLeft'].values\n   500→    trial_in_block_all = compute_trial_in_block(prob_left_all)\n   501→    # Apply masks to get trial_in_block for valid+combined trials\n   502→    trial_in_block_valid = trial_in_block_all[mask.values]\n   503→    trial_in_block_final = trial_in_block_valid[combined_mask]\n   504→\n   505→    input_trials = []\n   506→    for t in range(len(final_trials)):\n   507→        inp = np.array([\n   508→            time_since_stim,  # time-varying\n   509→            np.full(N_BINS, trial_in_block_final[t], dtype=np.float32),  # per-trial, broadcast to time\n   510→        ], dtype=np.float32)  # (2, n_bins)\n   511→        input_trials.append(inp)\n   512→\n   513→    # Build outputs\n   514→    # Output 0: choice (binary, per-trial) - left=0, right=1\n   515→    choice = final_trials['choice'].values.copy()\n   516→    # IBL: -1=left, 1=right -> convert to 0=left, 1=right\n   517→    choice_encoded = ((choice + 1) // 2).astype(int)  # -1->0, 1->1\n   518→\n   519→    # Output 1: prior probability of left (per-trial, categorical)\n   520→    prob_left = final_trials['probabilityLeft'].values\n   521→    prior_encoded = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=int)\n   522→    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.2] = 0\n   523→    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.5] = 1\n   524→    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.8] = 2\n   525→\n   526→    # Output 2: wheel speed discretized into 3 bins (time-varying)\n   527→    # Discretize using quantiles across ALL timepoints in this session\n   528→    all_wheel_flat = final_wheel.flatten()\n   529→    wheel_disc, wheel_boundaries = discretize_to_bins(all_wheel_flat, N_DISCRETE_BINS)\n   530→    wheel_disc_2d = wheel_disc.reshape(final_wheel.shape).astype(int)\n   531→\n   532→    # Output 3: whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins (time-varying)\n   533→    all_me_flat = final_me.flatten()\n   534→    me_disc, me_boundaries = discretize_to_bins(all_me_flat, N_DISCRETE_BINS)\n   535→    me_disc_2d = me_disc.reshape(final_me.shape).astype(int)\n   536→\n   537→    output_trials = []\n   538→    for t in range(len(final_trials)):\n   539→        out = np.array([\n   540→            np.full(N_BINS, choice_encoded[t], dtype=int),  # per-trial, broadcast\n   541→            np.full(N_BINS, prior_encoded[t], dtype=int),  # per-trial, broadcast\n   542→            wheel_disc_2d[t],  # time-varying\n   543→            me_disc_2d[t],  # time-varying\n   544→        ], dtype=int)  # (4, n_bins)\n   545→        output_trials.append(out)\n   546→\n   547→    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n   548→    print(f\"  Session {eid} ({subject}/{date}): {n_valid} trials, {n_clusters} neurons, {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n   549→\n   550→    # Processing visualization\n   551→    if show_processing:\n   552→        try:\n   553→            plot_processing(eid, subject, date, final_spikes, final_wheel, final_me,\n   554→                          wheel_disc_2d, me_disc_2d, choice_encoded, prior_encoded,\n   555→                          time_since_stim, cluster_regions)\n   556→        except Exception as e:\n   557→            print(f\"  Warning: could not create processing plots: {e}\")\n   558→\n   559→    return {\n   560→        'neural': neural_trials,\n   561→        'input': input_trials,\n   562→        'output': output_trials,\n   563→        'subject': subject,\n   564→        'cluster_regions': cluster_regions,\n   565→        'n_clusters': n_clusters,\n   566→        'n_trials': n_valid,\n   567→        'eid': eid,\n   568→    }\n   569→\n   570→\n   571→def plot_processing(eid, subject, date, spikes, wheel, me, wheel_disc, me_disc,\n   572→                   choice, prior, time_axis, cluster_regions):\n   573→    \"\"\"Plot processing steps for visual verification.\"\"\"\n   574→    import matplotlib\n   575→    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n   576→    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n   577→\n   578→    fig, axes = plt.subplots(4, 3, figsize=(18, 16))\n   579→    fig.suptitle(f'Processing: {subject}/{date} ({eid[:8]}...)', fontsize=14)\n   580→\n   581→    # Trial 0 and 1\n   582→    for col, trial_idx in enumerate([0, min(1, len(spikes)-1)]):\n   583→        # Neural activity (first 50 neurons)\n   584→        n_show = min(50, spikes.shape[1])\n   585→        axes[0, col].imshow(spikes[trial_idx, :n_show, :], aspect='auto', cmap='hot',\n   586→                           extent=[time_axis[0], time_axis[-1], n_show, 0])\n   587→        axes[0, col].set_title(f'Trial {trial_idx}: Neural ({n_show}/{spikes.shape[1]} neurons)')\n   588→        axes[0, col].set_xlabel('Time from stimOn (s)')\n   589→\n   590→        # Wheel speed (raw vs discretized)\n   591→        axes[1, col].plot(time_axis, wheel[trial_idx], 'b-', alpha=0.5, label='Raw wheel speed')\n   592→        axes[1, col].plot(time_axis, wheel_disc[trial_idx], 'r-', label='Discretized')\n   593→        axes[1, col].set_title(f'Trial {trial_idx}: Wheel speed')\n   594→        axes[1, col].legend(fontsize=8)\n   595→\n   596→        # Whisker ME (raw vs discretized)\n   597→        axes[2, col].plot(time_axis, me[trial_idx], 'b-', alpha=0.5, label='Raw ME')\n   598→        axes[2, col].plot(time_axis, me_disc[trial_idx], 'r-', label='Discretized')\n   599→        axes[2, col].set_title(f'Trial {trial_idx}: Whisker ME')\n   600→        axes[2, col].legend(fontsize=8)\n   601→\n   602→    # Summary statistics\n   603→    # Choice distribution\n   604→    axes[0, 2].bar(['Left (0)', 'Right (1)'],\n   605→                   [np.sum(choice == 0), np.sum(choice == 1)])\n   606→    axes[0, 2].set_title('Choice distribution')\n   607→\n   608→    # Prior distribution\n   609→    axes[1, 2].bar(['0.2 (0)', '0.5 (1)', '0.8 (2)'],\n   610→                   [np.sum(prior == 0), np.sum(prior == 1), np.sum(prior == 2)])\n   611→    axes[1, 2].set_title('Prior distribution')\n   612→\n   613→    # Wheel speed histogram\n   614→    axes[2, 2].hist(wheel.flatten(), bins=50, alpha=0.7)\n   615→    axes[2, 2].set_title('Wheel speed distribution')\n   616→\n   617→    # Brain regions\n   618→    unique_regions, counts = np.unique(cluster_regions, return_counts=True)\n   619→    sort_idx = np.argsort(-counts)[:15]  # Top 15\n   620→    axes[3, 0].barh(range(len(sort_idx)), counts[sort_idx])\n   621→    axes[3, 0].set_yticks(range(len(sort_idx)))\n   622→    axes[3, 0].set_yticklabels(unique_regions[sort_idx], fontsize=7)\n   623→    axes[3, 0].set_title('Top brain regions')\n   624→\n   625→    # ME distribution\n   626→    axes[3, 1].hist(me.flatten(), bins=50, alpha=0.7)\n   627→    axes[3, 1].set_title('Whisker ME distribution')\n   628→\n   629→    # Mean neural activity across trials\n   630→    axes[3, 2].plot(time_axis, spikes.mean(axis=(0, 1)), 'k-')\n   631→    axes[3, 2].set_title('Mean firing rate')\n   632→    axes[3, 2].set_xlabel('Time from stimOn (s)')\n   633→\n   634→    fig.tight_layout()\n   635→    safe_eid = eid[:8]\n   636→    fig.savefig(f'/app/processing_{safe_eid}.png', dpi=150)\n   637→    plt.close(fig)\n   638→    print(f\"  Saved processing plot: processing_{safe_eid}.png\")\n   639→\n   640→\n   641→def get_session_list(bwm_df):\n   642→    \"\"\"Get unique sessions from BWM release CSV.\"\"\"\n   643→    # Group by eid to get one row per session\n   644→    sessions = bwm_df.groupby('eid').first().reset_index()\n   645→    return sessions\n   646→\n   647→\n   648→def main():\n   649→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n   650→    parser.add_argument('output', type=str, help='Output pickle file path')\n   651→    parser.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True, help='Process all sessions (default)')\n   652→    parser.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions for testing')\n   653→    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Plot processing visualizations')\n   654→    args = parser.parse_args()\n   655→\n   656→    if args.sample:\n   657→        args.full = False\n   658→\n   659→    print(\"=\" * 60)\n   660→    print(\"IBL Brain-Wide Map Data Conversion\")\n   661→    print(\"=\" * 60)\n   662→\n   663→    t_start = time.time()\n   664→\n   665→    # Load BWM release info\n   666→    bwm_df = pd.read_csv(BWM_CSV, index_col=0)\n   667→    sessions = get_session_list(bwm_df)\n   668→    print(f\"Total sessions in BWM release: {len(sessions)}\")\n   669→\n   670→    if args.sample:\n   671→        # Pick 2 sessions for testing\n   672→        np.random.seed(42)\n   673→        sample_idx = np.random.choice(len(sessions), min(2, len(sessions)), replace=False)\n   674→        sessions = sessions.iloc[sample_idx].reset_index(drop=True)\n   675→        print(f\"Sample mode: processing {len(sessions)} sessions\")\n   676→\n   677→    # Initialize brain atlas\n   678→    br = BrainRegions()\n   679→\n   680→    # Process sessions\n   681→    all_results = []\n   682→    for idx in range(len(sessions)):\n   683→        sess = sessions.iloc[idx]\n   684→        print(f\"\\nProcessing session {idx+1}/{len(sessions)}: {sess['subject']}/{sess['date']}\")\n   685→        result = process_session(sess, br, show_processing=args.show_processing)\n   686→        if result is not None:\n   687→            all_results.append(result)\n   688→\n   689→        if (idx + 1) % 10 == 0:\n   690→            elapsed = time.time() - t_start\n   691→            rate = elapsed / (idx + 1)\n   692→            remaining = rate * (len(sessions) - idx - 1)\n   693→            print(f\"\\n  Progress: {idx+1}/{len(sessions)} sessions, \"\n   694→                  f\"{elapsed:.0f}s elapsed, ~{remaining:.0f}s remaining\")\n   695→\n   696→    print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n   697→    print(f\"Successfully processed {len(all_results)} / {len(sessions)} sessions\")\n   698→\n   699→    if len(all_results) == 0:\n   700→        print(\"ERROR: No sessions processed successfully!\")\n   701→        sys.exit(1)\n   702→\n   703→    # Build final data structure\n   704→    print(\"\\nBuilding final data structure...\")\n   705→\n   706→    # Collect all unique subjects\n   707→    all_subjects = sorted(set(r['subject'] for r in all_results))\n   708→    subject_to_idx = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(all_subjects)}\n   709→\n   710→    # Collect all unique brain regions\n   711→    all_regions_set = set()\n   712→    for r in all_results:\n   713→        all_regions_set.update(r['cluster_regions'])\n   714→    all_regions = sorted(all_regions_set)\n   715→    region_to_idx = {r: i for i, r in enumerate(all_regions)}\n   716→\n   717→    # Build lists\n   718→    neural_list = []\n   719→    input_list = []\n   720→    output_list = []\n   721→    subject_idx = []\n   722→    brain_region_idx = []\n   723→\n   724→    for r in all_results:\n   725→        neural_list.append(r['neural'])\n   726→        input_list.append(r['input'])\n   727→        output_list.append(r['output'])\n   728→        subject_idx.append(subject_to_idx[r['subject']])\n   729→        brain_region_idx.append(np.array([region_to_idx[reg] for reg in r['cluster_regions']]))\n   730→\n   731→    data = {\n   732→        'neural': neural_list,\n   733→        'input': input_list,\n   734→        'output': output_list,\n   735→        'subjects': all_subjects,\n   736→        'subject_idx': np.array(subject_idx),\n   737→        'brain_regions': all_regions,\n   738→        'brain_region_idx': brain_region_idx,\n   739→        'input_names': ['time_since_stimulus_onset', 'trial_number_in_block'],\n   740→        'output_names': ['choice', 'prior_probability_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy'],\n   741→        'output_values': [\n   742→            ['left', 'right'],                  # choice: 0=left, 1=right\n   743→            ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'],              # prior: 0=0.2, 1=0.5, 2=0.8\n   744→            ['low', 'medium', 'high'],           # wheel speed: 3 quantile bins\n   745→            ['low', 'medium', 'high'],           # whisker ME: 3 quantile bins\n   746→        ],\n   747→        'metadata': {\n   748→            'task_description': 'IBL decision-making task: mice rotate wheel to indicate location of visual stimulus',\n   749→            'time_bin_size': BINSIZE * 1000,  # in ms\n   750→            'temporal_alignment_event': 'stimulus onset (stimOn_times)',\n   751→            'off_start': TIME_WINDOW[0],\n   752→            'off_end': TIME_WINDOW[1],\n   753→            'n_timepoints': N_BINS,\n   754→            'dataset': 'IBL Brain-Wide Map',\n   755→            'n_sessions': len(all_results),\n   756→            'n_subjects': len(all_subjects),\n   757→            'total_trials': sum(r['n_trials'] for r in all_results),\n   758→            'total_neurons': sum(r['n_clusters'] for r in all_results),\n   759→        }\n   760→    }\n   761→\n   762→    # Print summary\n   763→    print(f\"\\nDataset Summary:\")\n   764→    print(f\"  Sessions: {len(all_results)}\")\n   765→    print(f\"  Subjects: {len(all_subjects)}\")\n   766→    print(f\"  Total trials: {data['metadata']['total_trials']}\")\n   767→    print(f\"  Total neurons: {data['metadata']['total_neurons']}\")\n   768→    print(f\"  Brain regions: {len(all_regions)}\")\n   769→    print(f\"  Time bins: {N_BINS} ({BINSIZE*1000:.0f} ms each)\")\n   770→    print(f\"  Inputs: {data['input_names']}\")\n   771→    print(f\"  Outputs: {data['output_names']}\")\n   772→\n   773→    # Check revision directories (format: #YYYY-MM-DD#)\n    candidates = []\n    if base_path.exists():\n        for item in base_path.iterdir():\n            if item.is_dir() and item.name.startswith('#') and item.name.endswith('#'):\n                for f in item.iterdir():\n                    if filename_pattern in f.name:\n                        candidates.append(f)\n        # Also check base path directly\n        for f in base_path.iterdir():\n            if not f.is_dir() and filename_pattern in f.name:\n                candidates.append(f)\n\n    if not candidates:\n        return None\n    # Sort by revision date (newest first), files without revision come last\n    def sort_key(p):\n        parts = p.parts\n        for part in parts:\n            if part.startswith('#') and part.endswith('#'):\n                return part\n        return '#0000-00-00#'\n    candidates.sort(key=sort_key, reverse=True)\n    return candidates[0]\n\n\ndef load_trials(alf_path):\n    \"\"\"Load trials table from session.\"\"\"\n    trials_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_trials.table.pqt')\n    if trials_file is None:\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No trials table found in {alf_path}\")\n    trials = pd.read_parquet(trials_file)\n    return trials\n\n\ndef create_trial_mask(trials):\n    \"\"\"Create boolean mask for trial inclusion following reference code.\"\"\"\n    mask = pd.Series(True, index=trials.index)\n\n    # Exclude NaN in key events\n    for event in NAN_EXCLUDE:\n        if event in trials.columns:\n            mask &= ~trials[event].isna()\n\n    # Reaction time filter\n    rt = trials['firstMovement_times'] - trials['stimOn_times']\n    mask &= (rt >= MIN_RT) & (rt <= MAX_RT)\n\n    # Map cluster to region via channel assignment\n    cluster_regions = chan_beryl[clusters['channels']]\n    return cluster_regions\n\n\ndef bin_spikes_vectorized(spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, interval_starts, interval_ends, binsize, n_bins):\n    \"\"\"Bin spikes into trial-aligned time windows. Optimized implementation.\"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n    binned = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n\n    # Pre-sort spikes are already time-sorted after merge\n    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n\n        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n            continue\n\n        # Use searchsorted for fast interval selection\n        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_start, side='left')\n        idx_end = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        if idx_beg >= idx_end:\n            continue\n\n        t_sel = spike_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n        c_sel = spike_clusters[idx_beg:idx_end]\n\n        # Compute bin indices\n        bin_idx = np.minimum(((t_sel - t_start) / binsize).astype(np.int32), n_bins - 1)\n\n        # Use flat indexing for efficient accumulation\n        valid = (c_sel >= 0) & (c_sel < n_clusters)\n        flat_idx = c_sel[valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n        np.add.at(binned[trial_idx].ravel(), flat_idx, 1)\n\n    return binned\n\n\ndef interpolate_wheel(timestamps, position, fs=WHEEL_FS, corner_freq=WHEEL_CORNER_FREQ, order=WHEEL_FILTER_ORDER):\n    \"\"\"Interpolate wheel position and compute velocity (matching ibllib).\"\"\"\n    t = np.arange(timestamps[0], timestamps[-1], 1.0 / fs)\n    if len(t) > 0 and t[-1] > timestamps[-1]:\n        t = t[:-1]\n\n    pos_interp = interp1d(timestamps, position, kind='linear')(t)\n\n    # Butterworth low-pass filter for velocity\n    sos = signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_freq / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\n    vel = np.insert(np.diff(signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos_interp)), 0, 0) * fs\n\n    return t, np.abs(vel).astype(np.float32)  # wheel speed = |velocity|\n\n\ndef load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left'):\n    \"\"\"Load whisker motion energy from camera data.\"\"\"\n    if side == 'left':\n        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')\n    else:\n        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n\n    if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n        return None, None\n\n    me = np.load(me_file).flatten()\n    times = np.load(times_file).flatten()\n\n    # Handle length mismatch (common in IBL data)\n    min_len = min(len(me), len(times))\n    return times[:min_len], me[:min_len].astype(np.float32)\n\n\ndef interpolate_behavior_to_bins(beh_times, beh_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, binsize, n_bins):\n    \"\"\"Interpolate behavior signal to trial-aligned bins (matching reference code).\"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n    result = np.full((n_trials, n_bins), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    good_mask = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n\n    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n\n        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n            continue\n\n        # Find behavior data in this interval\n        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_start, side='right')\n        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        t_sel = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n        v_sel = beh_values[idx_beg:idx_end]\n\n        if len(v_sel) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Check data availability (matching reference code)\n        if np.abs(t_start - t_sel[0]) > binsize:\n            continue\n        if np.abs(t_end - t_sel[-1]) > binsize:\n            continue\n\n        # Interpolate to uniform bins (matching reference code)\n        x_interp = np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n        try:\n            y_interp = interp1d(t_sel, v_sel, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n            result[trial_idx] = y_interp\n            good_mask[trial_idx] = True\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n\n    return result, good_mask\n\n\ndef discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=N_DISCRETE_BINS):\n    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins equal-frequency bins using quantiles.\n    Computed across all valid (non-NaN) values.\"\"\"\n    valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n    if len(valid) == 0:\n        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=int), np.array([])\n\n    # Compute quantile boundaries\n    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n    boundaries = np.percentile(valid, quantiles)\n\n    # Digitize - np.digitize returns values 0..n_bins, clip to 0..n_bins-1\n    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries[1:-1])  # 0 to n_bins-1\n    result = np.clip(result, 0, n_bins - 1)\n\n    return result.astype(int), boundaries\n\n\ndef compute_trial_in_block(prob_left):\n    \"\"\"Compute trial number within each block.\n    Block boundaries are where probabilityLeft changes.\"\"\"\n    trial_in_block = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    count = 1\n    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n        if i > 0 and prob_left[i] != prob_left[i-1]:\n            count = 1\n        trial_in_block[i] = count\n        count += 1\n    return trial_in_block\n\n\ndef process_session(session_info, br, show_processing=False):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\"\"\"\n    eid = session_info['eid']\n    subject = session_info['subject']\n    lab = session_info['lab']\n    date = session_info['date']\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    # Find session path\n    alf_path = find_session_path(lab, subject, date)\n    if alf_path is None:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid} ({subject}/{date}): path not found, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    # Load trials\n    try:\n        trials = load_trials(alf_path)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading trials: {e}\")\n        return None\n\n    # Create trial mask\n    mask = create_trial_mask(trials)\n\n    # Load and merge spike sorting from all probes\n    spikes_list = []\n    clusters_list = []\n    for probe_name in probe_dirs:\n        try:\n            spk, clu = load_spike_sorting(alf_path, probe_name)\n            spikes_list.append(spk)\n            clusters_list.append(clu)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  Session {eid}, {probe_name}: error loading spikes: {e}\")\n            continue\n\n    if not spikes_list:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: no valid probes, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    spikes, clusters = merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)\n    n_clusters = len(clusters['channels'])\n\n    # Get brain regions\n    cluster_regions = get_brain_regions(clusters, br)\n\n    # Apply trial mask\n    valid_trials = trials[mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    if len(valid_trials) < 2:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: fewer than 2 valid trials, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    # Compute intervals aligned to stimOn_times\n    align_times = valid_trials[ALIGN_TIME].values\n    interval_starts = align_times + TIME_WINDOW[0]\n    interval_ends = align_times + TIME_WINDOW[1]\n\n    # Bin spikes\n    binned_spikes = bin_spikes_vectorized(\n        spikes['times'], spikes['clusters'], n_clusters,\n        interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n    )\n    # binned_spikes shape: (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n\n    # Load wheel data\n    wheel_speed_binned = None\n    try:\n        wheel_pos_raw = np.load(find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_wheel.position.npy')).flatten()\n        wheel_ts_raw = np.load(find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy')).flatten()\n        wheel_times, wheel_speed = interpolate_wheel(wheel_ts_raw, wheel_pos_raw)\n        wheel_speed_binned, wheel_good = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n            wheel_times, wheel_speed, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n        )\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading wheel: {e}\")\n\n    # Load whisker motion energy\n    me_binned = None\n    try:\n        me_times, me_values = load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left')\n        if me_times is None:\n            me_times, me_values = load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='right')\n        if me_times is not None:\n            me_binned, me_good = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n                me_times, me_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n            )\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading motion energy: {e}\")\n\n    # Build combined mask (trials where all data is available)\n    combined_mask = np.ones(len(valid_trials), dtype=bool)\n    if wheel_speed_binned is not None:\n        combined_mask &= ~np.any(np.isnan(wheel_speed_binned), axis=1)\n    else:\n        # If no wheel data, mark all as bad\n        combined_mask[:] = False\n\n    if me_binned is not None:\n        combined_mask &= ~np.any(np.isnan(me_binned), axis=1)\n    else:\n        combined_mask[:] = False\n\n    # (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n    final_wheel = wheel_speed_binned[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n    final_me = me_binned[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n    final_trials = valid_trials[combined_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    # Build neural data: list of (n_neurons, n_timepoints) per trial\n    # Use uint8 to save memory (spike counts per 20ms bin are small integers, max ~12)\n    neural_trials = []\n    for t in range(len(final_spikes)):\n        neural_trials.append(final_spikes[t].astype(np.uint8))  # (n_clusters, n_bins)\n\n    # Build inputs\n    # Input 0: time since stimulus onset (continuous, time-varying)\n    time_since_stim = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS).astype(np.float32)\n\n    # Input 1: trial number in block (continuous, per-trial)\n    prob_left_all = trials['probabilityLeft'].values\n    trial_in_block_all = compute_trial_in_block(prob_left_all)\n    # Apply masks to get trial_in_block for valid+combined trials\n    trial_in_block_valid = trial_in_block_all[mask.values]\n    trial_in_block_final = trial_in_block_valid[combined_mask]\n\n    input_trials = []\n    for t in range(len(final_trials)):\n        inp = np.array([\n            time_since_stim,  # time-varying\n            np.full(N_BINS, trial_in_block_final[t], dtype=np.float32),  # per-trial, broadcast to time\n        ], dtype=np.float32)  # (2, n_bins)\n        input_trials.append(inp)\n\n    # Build outputs\n    # Output 0: choice (binary, per-trial) - left=0, right=1\n    choice = final_trials['choice'].values.copy()\n    # IBL: -1=left, 1=right -> convert to 0=left, 1=right\n    choice_encoded = ((choice + 1) // 2).astype(int)  # -1->0, 1->1\n\n    # Output 1: prior probability of left (per-trial, categorical)\n    prob_left = final_trials['probabilityLeft'].values\n    prior_encoded = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=int)\n    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.2] = 0\n    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.5] = 1\n    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.8] = 2\n\n    # Output 2: wheel speed discretized into 3 bins (time-varying)\n    # Discretize using quantiles across ALL timepoints in this session\n    all_wheel_flat = final_wheel.flatten()\n    wheel_disc, wheel_boundaries = discretize_to_bins(all_wheel_flat, N_DISCRETE_BINS)\n    wheel_disc_2d = wheel_disc.reshape(final_wheel.shape).astype(int)\n\n    # {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DbKNzMVJ28EcUCxscxq3Nh","type":"tool_result","content":"=== Step 18: Read methods.txt ===\n{\n  \"file_path\": \"/app/methods.txt\"\n}\n1→From \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\"\n     2→\n     3→Our goal is to decode behavior y \\in R^P from spike-sorted, temporally binned spike counts. For each trial k, neural activity has size N x T, where N is the number of neurons and T the number of time steps. Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps. For each trial, we constructed X \\in R^{N x T}  by aggregating spike counts and used it to compute a decoder estimate of the true behavior y \\in R^P. y is constant within a trial (“per-trial decoding”); when P = T, y varies across time (“per-timestep decoding”).\n     4→\n     5→We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions,17 covering 270 brain regions and four behavioral variables: choice, prior, wheel speed, and whisker motion energy.\n     6→\n     7→ In the IBL experiments, mice rotate a wheel to indicate the location of a visual stimulus, defined as their choice (Figure 1A). For the first 90 trials, the stimulus appears randomly on either side with equal probability; in subsequent trials, it appears predominantly on one side in blocks. Mice adapt their behavior to these changing probabilities, allowing estimation of each mouse’s trial-by-trial prior belief (prior) about stimulus location. Additionally, wheel speed and whisker motion energy near the whisker pad are recorded. Whisker motion energy is computed as the mean absolute difference between adjacent video frames within a bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye. Choice and prior are static within a trial, while wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz. See STAR Methods sections “data processing” and “hyperparameter selection” for details.\n     8→\n     9→Data processing\n    10→\n    11→IBL trial-aligned dataset\n    12→\n    13→For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset. For prior, we also align trials to the stimulus onset, including neural activity from 0.6 s to 0.1 s pre-onset. The prior represents the mice’s estimate of the stimulus side probability. We use the same decoding window as in the previous study,27 focusing on the period with minimal wheel movements. Within each trial, we segment neural activity into 50-ms non-overlapping time bins. For each time bin, we bin spike counts using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5,77 from each session. For dynamic behaviors (wheel speed and whisker motion energy), we select the first movement onset as the alignment event, and decode the behavior starting at the alignment event and ending at 1 s after the alignment event. The neural activity within each trial is binned into non-overlapping 20 ms bins. For each time bin, we similarly bin spike counts using all neurons from each session.\n    14→\n    15→IBL trial-unaligned dataset\n    16→\n    17→We focus only on wheel speed and whisker motion energy, as variables like choice and prior are only defined during experimental trials, which are periods when stimuli are presented and mice respond for rewards. Unlike the trial-aligned dataset, we do not align neural activity and behavior to a specific event onset. Instead, we segment the continuous Neuropixels recordings into 2-s snippets to construct the trial-unaligned dataset for each session. These snippets cover intervals both during and outside experimental trials, capturing the repetitive, structured behaviors within trials as well as the more spontaneous behaviors outside of trials.\n    18→\n    19→From \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\"\n    20→\n    21→We trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female) on the International Brain Laboratory (IBL) decision-making task (Fig. 1a,b). On each trial, a visual stimulus appeared to the left or right on a screen, and the mouse had to move it to the centre by turning a wheel with its front paws within 60 s (Fig. 1c). After an initial 90 unbiased trials, the prior probability for the stimulus to appear on the left or right side was constant over a block of trials at a ratio of 20:80% (right block) or 80:20% (left block). Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials, which were drawn from a truncated geometric distribution (empirical mean of 51 trials). Block changes were not cued. Stimulus contrast was uniformly sampled from 5 possible values (100, 25, 12.5, 6.25 and 0%). The 0% contrast trials, when no stimulus was presented, were assigned to a left or right side following the probability distribution of the block. This allowed mice to perform above chance by incorporating this prior in their choices. Following a wheel turn, mice received positive feedback in the form of a water reward, or negative feedback in the form of a white-noise pulse and a 2-s time out. The next trial began after a delay, followed by a quiescence period during which the mice had to hold the wheel still.\n    22→\n    23→Recordings\n    24→\n    25→In these mice,","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01BTFa3Me3ZBHcBrTCdJhf5s","session_id":"621b3579-4913-413d-abc7-9ef3a4534867","uuid":"07f5b072-3a2d-48be-9984-46f788455f9c","timestamp":"2026-08-03T14:37:18.467Z"}
